Cantor's **$125–130B RPO** ➜ **wrong by ~$21B on the reported line**, though the $104B + $25B early-Q3 commits gets there off-balance-sheet. DB's warning that capex-ahead-of-revenue **"limits opportunity for upside to non-GAAP operating margins"** ➜ **wrong this quarter**; that is the single biggest perception change in the print.

**🆕 2026-08-11 (pre-print) — JPM initiates a Dec-27 target: NEUTRAL, PT $110 (raised from Dec-26 $105) (JPM / Samik Chatterjee, 2026-08-11).** Constructive on demand and on CRWV's progression toward *"an increasingly relevant PaaS vendor with high-margin hardware and software attach"*; cites **[[SPCX]] compute-deal pricing (its contracts with [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[GOOG]]) as evidence of a supply-demand imbalance he forecasts to WIDEN in the medium term.** The reason he stays Neutral is precise and now testable: *"we see limited near-term catalysts with our forecast for **F3Q Op. margins of 7.0% vs. consensus of 7.5%**."* He also frames the overbuild rebuttal the way the bulls do — CRWV **builds only against committed customers and contracts supply to limit input-price risk**, which caps downside but *"do limit the magnitude of upside to near-term forecasts."* ⚠️ **Note the tension with the print: Q2 adj. OM came in ~5% vs a ~3% bogey, i.e. the margin scepticism JPM anchors on was already loosening as the note published.**

**🆕 2026-08-10 — prints Tue 2026-08-11 post-close; BofA into the print, Buy PO $140 (BofA / Tal Liani, preview dated 2026-07-20, relayed by Brian Fenske 2026-08-10).**
- **The one operating number the preview hangs on:** *"CRWV is targeting an increase in **active power from approximately 1.0 GW to 1.7 GW by year-end**, with more capacity expected to come online in **2H26** and drive **accelerating revenue**."* ⚠️ **Active power, not contracted — this is the metric the wiki should track against the contracted-GW figures elsewhere on this page; they are different denominators.**
- **What the desk says to focus on:** *"demand commentary, capacity rollouts / any execution issues (and success of course), margins, capex required, and customer wins/diversification."*
- **Flow/positioning colour (BofA Equity Trading Flows, through 2026-08-07):** *"**LOs have been buyers across the BofA platform, broadly, YTD, even during the big drawdown**."* ⚠️ Single-broker platform flow, not market-wide — directional only.

Q1 FY2026 (call 2026-05-07): revenue **$2.1B, +112% YoY / +32% QoQ**; Adjusted EBITDA **$1.2B (~56% margin)**; Adjusted Operating Income only **$21M (~1% margin)**; net loss **$(740)M** (vs. $(315)M PY) as interest expense hit **$536M** (10-Q Q1-26, 2026-05-08; transcript 2026-05-07). Revenue backlog **$99.4B**, +~50% QoQ (~4x YoY), with $40B+ new bookings in Q1 and 10 customers at $1B+ commitments — fueled in part by a **$21B agreement with Meta Platforms** — and ~75% of 2027's >$30B guide already contracted. Active power crossed **1 GW**; contracted power **3.5+ GW**; on track for 1.7+ GW active by year-end 2026. Capex guide raised to **$31B–$35B** for FY2026 on component pricing, funded primarily through debt (Barron's "Inside Scoop," 2026-06-01). FY2026 revenue reaffirmed **$12B–$13B**, Adjusted Operating Income **$900M–$1.1B**, exit ARR raised to **$18B–$19B**. >50% of compute now used for inference (Intrator). Balance sheet: total principal debt **$25.1B** at Mar-31 (effective rates 10–15% across DDTL facilities), operating-lease liabilities **$10.1B** ROU assets, cash $3.3B (10-Q Q1-26).

**Press/tape read (Barron's, 2026-06-01):** "Inside Scoop" restates the same quarter in blunter terms — "CoreWeave lost $740 million in the first quarter—more than double that of a year ago and the largest in CoreWeave's history as a public company" — alongside the $99.4B backlog / $21B Meta agreement as the bull anchor, with the stock "jumped 45% this year" as of that issue (Barron's, 2026-06-01).

## Unit economics / ROIC (DB CRWV unit economics, 16 Jun 2025)
DB walked through the company's illustrative contract (a 4yr $5bn TCV deal + 2 incremental years of asset useful-life monetization) and its own per-GPU model, concluding current contract economics are **sustainable**: CapEx is "more than recouped within the initial 4-year term" including cost of capital, with the majority of equity NPV captured in years 5–6 of GPU life (DB CRWV unit economics, 16 Jun 2025).
- **Revenue yield:** company example ~**$0.44 of annual revenue per $1 of capex** over the initial 4yr contract; DB's own estimate slightly lower at ~$0.40 (partly offset by a 15–20% TCV upfront prepayment).
- **Contract EBITDA margin ~80%** in the company example (DB framework 78%); contracts span ~65–90% run-rate depending on size/duration/prepayment. The reported corporate Adj. EBITDA margin was a lower 64% in CY24 — delta is R&D/S&M/G&A overhead plus timing of standing up new capacity (OpEx ex-D&A is MSD% of revenue, trending down).
- **Payback ~2.5yrs** (incl. prepayment) on committed contracts at CYE24; DB models a ~breakeven NPV on the initial 4yr contract and **positive NPV ~$0.30 per $1 of net capex over a 6yr useful life** — i.e., value creation sits in the tail (re-contracting / on-demand).
- **Re-contracting:** company assumes capacity re-leased at **75% of original ASP** in years 5–6; DB more conservative at 50% (A100 precedent). Sensitivity: NPV/$ net capex ranges ~$0.12–$0.44 at a 10% WACC across $1.90–$2.40 /GPU/hr.
- **Cost of capital:** company example used 85% loan-to-cost and **9% cost of debt** (trending down) vs. DB's 10% WACC; effective rate on existing debt is >10%. $3bn+ NOLs limit near-term cash taxes (DB argues a tax allowance still belongs in unit economics).
- DB rated **Hold, PT $50** (16 Jun 2025) — its open questions are longer-term: contract structure as the base broadens, supply normalizes, hyperscalers close the leading-edge gap, and whether AI app monetization sustains the capex.

## Debate / thesis
- **Bull:** Demand far exceeds supply — "largely sold out of 2026 capacity with prices increasing across the board from Ampere to Hopper to Blackwell... extending into 2027" (CFO, via @TheTranscript_, briefing 2026-05-16). Backlog $99.4B and NVIDIA preference (Exemplar Cloud, $2B NVDA strategic investment Jan-2026 at $87.20, plus an additional $2B per @The_AI_Investor, briefing 2026-06-13) give multi-year visibility. Independent validation: CoreWeave sits at the **top of SemiAnalysis's ClusterMAX Gold tier**, ahead of Nebius, Oracle and Crusoe — "a case-study of a player with no initial scale winning over the market by executing perfectly on speed, site selection and financing" (SemiAnalysis Microsoft AI Strategy, Dec 2025). Microsoft, after under-building, is now "forced to go with the worst option: renting GPUs from Neoclouds and reselling at materially lower-than-usual margins" — direct confirmation of neocloud pricing power and that hyperscalers are short near-term capacity (SemiAnalysis Microsoft AI Strategy, Dec 2025). Added to Nasdaq-100 June 2026 (briefings 2026-06-13/17) → passive flow. NVDA 13F holds 47.21M CRWV shares — public ecosystem endorsement (briefing 2026-05-16 morning).
- **Bear:** Bernstein reiterates **Underperform, TP $67** in its 1Q26 hyperscaler review — while CRWV has shown impressive growth standing up a neocloud to monetize power assets, it is "much smaller than any of the other cloud providers," has **no structural right-to-win** (GPUs or otherwise) and is at a structural disadvantage vs hyperscalers long-term; may sign more deals in the next ~12 months but Bernstein has concerns on the **viability of the business model** and CRWV's role in the infra ecosystem. Bernstein frames CRWV as an "interim capacity provider" benefiting from near-term power/GPU scarcity but facing concentrated-customer, buyer-insourcing and structurally-constrained-return headwinds; 1Q26 adj. op margin just ~0.5% on the OpEx-revenue lag (~6-8wk fit-out), with steady-state margin guided 25-30% but reported margins "volatile and below steady-state for an extended period." Note the $8.5B DDTL 4.0 (first IG-rated financing, ~7%, backed solely by the Meta take-or-pay) is viewed as a bespoke "proof of concept," not a repeatable funding baseline (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).
- **Bear:** Bernstein **Underperform, PT $67** (Rezaei/Madison) — FY26 AOI guide $900M–$1.1B is "nearly unhittable per math": needs fit-out <8 weeks **and** stabilized margins >21%; blue-sky 6 weeks + 25% only reaches top of range (briefings 2026-05-08/12, 2026-05-16). Heavy fixed debt payments are a structural disadvantage vs. equity-funded hyperscalers — "Short: ORCL/CoreWeave encumbered by fixed debt payments vs GOOG's now-equity-funded flexibility" (Rothschild, briefing 2026-06-03). NVDA Vera Rubin / MI455 racks at CoreWeave "still Engineering/Quality Samples — 800G OSFP cages not even populated" (@SemiAnalysis_, briefings 2026-06-04/05) → near-term delivery/ramp risk. **Hedgeye is short CRWV (the "Neocloud Winter" call)** — short since 4/11/2025 at $43, thesis: "CoreWeave 2.0" contagion risk for the space, monetization per connected MW didn't improve in 2025 vs 2024, high cost inflation (memory, electricity, labor) caps margins, hyperscalers' internal pipeline + Bitcoin-miner competition lower the odds of another megadeal, and **for the first time in 3 months like-for-like neocloud GPU pricing fell** — a leading indicator of softer future contract pricing; de-rating expected on the liability surge (HE Global Technology BB, Dec 2025).
- **Bernstein Gen-AI Handbook — CRWV as the neocloud margin floor (2026-06-22, framework):** the 247-page Handbook uses CoreWeave as the **pure-play IaaS benchmark to argue hyperscalers should out-earn neoclouds**: it cites CRWV at **~24% GAAP gross margin in FY24** vs Oracle's guided 30-40% OCI AI GM and Microsoft's "significantly higher" AI margins (full AI-PaaS stack + data gravity). Constructive on the asset-intensity side — Bernstein notes CRWV "was able to drive an improvement" in asset intensity as it scaled — but the net framing is that scale + unique IP + PaaS mix accrue to the hyperscalers, leaving the pure-rental neocloud structurally lower-margin. Consistent with Bernstein's separate Underperform/$67 on the name (Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence", 2026-06-22). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_Gen_AI_Software.html)
- **JPM "AI Capex 2.0" (2026-06-16):** notes the AI buildout has so far "effectively only been financing the data-center boxes in public markets — **with the exception of term loans and bonds for neocloud CoreWeave**," positioning CRWV as the proof-of-concept for public GPU financing and naming it (with Iren) as a natural Leveraged-Loan home for amortizing 5-yr GPU paper. Two read-throughs for the bear depreciation case: (1) JPM cites a **rebounding Neocloud H100 rental price index** as "a very positive indicator that some of the draconian obsolescence assumptions people have been using are flawed" — supportive of CRWV's ~6yr schedule; (2) but JPM flags the structural **duration mismatch** (markets want 10yr+ paper vs ~3-7yr GPU economic life) and notes the **single largest Leveraged-Loan tranche is just ~$13bn** — i.e. the LevLoan market alone can't scale to neocloud GPU-financing needs (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **Day-0 inference proof point (SemiAnalysis "DeepSeek V4 Day 0→43", 2026-06-09):** when SemiAnalysis's own GB300 cluster was down for the DeepSeek V4 launch, **CoreWeave contributed two spare GB300 NVL72 racks** to the open-source effort and is named an InferenceX supporter — incidental confirmation CRWV's GB300 NVL72 fleet is operational and being used around the clock.
- **GPU depreciation debate:** CoreWeave depreciates GPUs over **~6 years** vs. ~4yr at hyperscalers, ~3yr crypto mining, ~1.5yr EDA servers — a longer schedule flatters near-term margins and is the crux of the bear case on reported profitability (Morgan Stanley AI Factories; MS ties the 6yr life to Keith Weiss's CRWV initiation). MS cross-checks CRWV's GB200 NVL72 rental at **~$10.5/GPU/hr** against AI-factory unit economics (a 100MW NVL72 factory shows >50% profit margin), arguing CRWV's pricing is consistent with profit-seeking utilization (MS AI Factories).
- **Where sell-side/buy-side stands:** Bernstein UP $67 and Hedgeye's neocloud short are the loudest bear views in-corpus; DB Hold/$50 sits in between (sound current economics, longer-term skepticism). Barclays (Ross Sandler) initiated/covers at **Equal Weight, PT $120** (price $99.54, +20.6% upside) — a more constructive middle-ground read than Bernstein/Hedgeye, though flagging Meta's cloud pivot as a fresh risk (Barclays, 2026-07-01). **Mizuho (Rakesh) Neutral, PT $110** (2026-07-11) — constructive on the neocloud-capex backdrop ($398B'27/$485B'28, share 22%→35% by '28) but rating the name Neutral, another middle-ground read between the Bernstein/Hedgeye bears and the demand bulls. NVDA stake / Nasdaq-100 inclusion / SemiAnalysis Gold-tier ranking are the offsetting positives. Lockup overhang flagged: 9.2M shares registered for Jane Street's $1B equity investment, lockup expires July 15 — potential supply overhang (briefing 2026-06-11).
- **Bernstein $/MW comp-set note (Madison/Gautam, 2026-07-01):** in a $/MW analysis spanning emerging AI infra and cloud names, Bernstein flags CIFR and WULF as standing out on leasing margins, and IREN as showing strong margins from vertically-integrated landlord economics — but notes IREN **lags CRWV and other cloud names on scale/enterprise build**. Read-through: CRWV is positioned as the **scale/enterprise-build leader** among this comparison set, even if pure-landlord names like IREN post better unit margins on a narrower base (Bernstein, 2026-07-01).
- **🆕🔴🔴 THE CREDIT MARKET NOW PRICES CRWV COUNTERPARTY RISK DIRECTLY, AND THE NUMBER IS 300–600bp — THIS IS THE MOST INFORMATIVE ITEM ON THIS PAGE ON THE CENTRAL BEAR (Morgan Stanley Global Credit Strategy · Fernanda Lima / Carolyn Campbell / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Vishwas Patkar / James Egan, "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20).** The page has spent four quarters arguing about CRWV's contract quality, residual values and offtake credit using equity-side opinion. MS supplies an **observable, market-set measure** instead: the yields on **physically comparable data-center project bonds, sorted by who the offtaker is.**
  - **CoreWeave-offtake project bonds: APLD 9.250 '30 (Ellendale, ND, 250MW) at 8.2% · CORZ 7.750 '31 (TX/GA/OK/NC, 589MW) at 11.5% · EDGCOM 7.500 '31 (Chicago/Atlanta, 114MW, CoreWeave/Alibaba) at 14.2% · ELKGVP 7.500 '31 (Chicago, IL, 72MW) at 12.0%.**
  - **Amazon-offtake project bonds in the same table: STNGRY 6.000 '31 (Wink, TX, 216MW) at 7.9% and (Andrews, TX, 70MW) at 8.8%.**
  - ➜ ***The credit market is charging 300–600bp more for CoreWeave offtake than for Amazon offtake on physically comparable projects.*** **That is a priced, falsifiable measure of exactly the counterparty gap Bernstein's "Hyperscalers are Situationships, Not Spouses" thesis asserts qualitatively — and it is a harder datapoint than any equity-side rating on this page.** ⚠️ **Read it as a COUNTERPARTY spread, not a CRWV cost of funds: these are the project companies' bonds, not CRWV's own paper. The landlord/host pays this, which is the same channel through which the "3-5% higher yield-on-cost on neocloud colo leases" (SemiAnalysis, 07-06) and the rising $/MW for approved capacity (Bernstein, 08-04) reach CRWV's cost of capacity.**
  - ✅ **THE OFFSET, ON CRWV'S OWN INSTRUMENT, AND IT IS THE LARGEST TIGHTENING IN MS'S LEVERAGED-LOAN TABLE: the CRWV TERM LOAN (S+550), priced 7/30/2026, maturing 9/30/2031, rated BB, yields 9.15% — spread 584 → 527, −57bp.** For calibration in the same table: **QTS (S+225), BBB−, 5.90%, 236→231; Lambda ("LAMBCM"), 8/12/2026, 6.65%, 303→287.** ⚠️ **NOT a supersession of the SemiAnalysis 07-06 mark on this page (CRWV 5-year UNSECURED ~10% / ≈600bp Z-spread) — different instrument, different seniority, different date. Both stand as points.** **And CRWV is now a EURO high-yield issuer: EUR HY issuance in MS's channel table is attributed to CRWV at 0 → 2, i.e. $2bn** (MS, "A Break from the Heat, For Now", 2026-08-14) — the follow-through on the inaugural eurobond management disclosed on the Q2 call.
  - **Market backdrop MS attaches to it, which is why "wider" is in the title: HQ hyperscalers ~30bp WIDER YTD and yet supply has not slowed — $54bn done in July, $41bn in August MTD, ~$445bn of issuance across credit channels YTD (vs $217bn for full-year 2025; FY26E $500–550bn) — with MS expecting a busy fall and continued spread widening. MS's 2027 capex estimates sit at ~$1.4tn (and MS notes these "capex estimates now include SPCX"), with 2027 combined capex estimates having NEARLY DOUBLED versus November 2025 estimates.** ⚠️⚠️ **NO PER-NAME CAPEX SPLIT WAS TAKEN FROM THIS NOTE: the exhibit is a column-interleaved chart whose per-name values cannot be read reliably from the text layer. Deliberately not attempted.** ➜ **Net for the thesis: the funding WINDOW is open and CRWV's own loan is tightening, while the market simultaneously prices its offtake 300–600bp behind Amazon's. Those are not contradictory — they are the bull and the bear, both quantified, on the same page for the first time.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USCREDIT_20260820_2103.html)
- **🆕🔴🔴 NET-NEW HOUSE, AND IT CARRIES THE HIGHEST PT THIS PAGE HAS EVER HELD — WITH ESTIMATES EXPLICITLY "MATERIALLY AHEAD OF CONSENSUS" (Arete Research "NEO Clouds: Greater Pricing Power", 2026-08-21; Andrew Beale / Alex Pound / Pierre-Marie d'Ornano / Frederik Strauss).** **BUY, PT $317 for '27E — RAISED from $303** (ref price $90 as of 19-Aug-26, mkt cap $49bn, implied upside +253%), DCF-based at **11% WACC / 3% PGR, less net debt and other liabilities**. Arete models **revenue 26% ahead of consensus in '28E and 37% ahead in '29E** and has CRWV implied at only **8.2x EV/operating income in '29E** (EV/Rev '28E 3.4x · EV/EBIT '28E 14.4x · EV/Rev '30E 1.8x · EV/EBIT '30E 5.4x). ⚠️ **One-dollar internal inconsistency in the source, recorded rather than resolved: the ticker table and the required-disclosure page both say the PT is "up from $303", while the CoreWeave section text says "increased to $317 from $301". $303 taken as the prior mark (two mentions to one, and it is the disclosure page).** ⚠️ **For calibration against everything else on this page: $317 is ~2.1x DB's $150, the previous high, and ~4.7x Bernstein's $67.**
  - 🔴 **THE MECHANISM IS PRICE, AND IT IS MANAGEMENT'S OWN JULY DISCLOSURE READ ONE STEP FURTHER: CoreWeave and [[NBIS]] are now achieving $20–25m/MW pricing ⚠️ — that is REVENUE per MW per annum (the compute selling price), NOT capex and NOT build cost — against the historic $10–15m/MW for five-year take-or-pay contracts with IG hyperscalers.** The new level is for **2–3-year deals to enterprises and AI natives**, i.e. a different contract shape, not a like-for-like uplift on the legacy book. **CoreWeave raised prices 25% in July** (disclosed at 2Q26 reporting) and pointed to **5–10ppt of margin expansion on new Vera Rubin business vs its prior 25% margin guide**. ➜ **Arete's inference is the analytically important part, and it is the cleanest answer yet to the "is this just cost pass-through?" objection: Vera Rubin NVL-72 racks cost an estimated 70–80% MORE than Blackwell (memory inflation + silicon content) and draw ~50% more power, implying ~20% higher GPU capex per MW — so a 5–10ppt MARGIN uplift means CRWV is capturing contractual price BEYOND capex inflation, not merely passing it through.** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE, stated once and binding: do NOT net the $20–25m/MW revenue figure against any $/GW or $/W build-cost anchor on this page, and do NOT chain it into a capex series. It is a selling price.**
  - 🔴 **THE RELATIVE-VALUE CALL, WHICH ARETE ITSELF FLAGS AS COUNTER-INTUITIVE: leverage to improving compute pricing ranks `IREN` (most levered) ➜ COREWEAVE ➜ [[NBIS]] (least).** The naive answer is that CRWV has the least leverage because it has by far the biggest order book (**$104bn backlog ≈ 1.7GW at an assumed $12m/MW five-year rate**). Arete's counter is a CAPITAL-STRUCTURE argument this page has spent four quarters approaching from the bear side: **because CoreWeave has by far the most developed contract-financing stack (the DDTL asset-backed/contractual facilities), its EV is far larger than Nebius's while its EQUITY value is currently LOWER, against slightly greater near-term unsold GW — so a given repricing of unsold capacity moves CRWV's equity more.** The metric: **unsold MW per $bn of market cap at YE28E — CRWV 50 · `IREN` 60 · [[NBIS]] 33** (YE29E: 81 · 127 · 53), with CRWV unsold **2.4GW (58% of fleet) at YE28E and 3.9GW (69%) at YE29E** off installed GPU power of **4.1GW YE28E → 5.6GW YE29E**, and "a clear path to 8GW of power by '30E". ➜ **The leverage the bears price as fragility is, on Arete's arithmetic, the same leverage that makes CRWV the higher-beta way to own rising compute prices — the mirror image of the 300–600bp counterparty spread above, not a contradiction of it.** ⚠️ **Arete's own timing caveat: NEOs are typically sold out, so business signed today lands in 2027 or later; CRWV's near-term route to the new pricing is MATURING GPU CONTRACTS, "mainly starting in 4Q26 and beyond."** (`IREN` has no wiki page — plain text by convention.)
  - **The supply-and-demand frame the PT rests on:** compute supply is unlikely to catch up soon — gated by **wafer and power constraints**, with **US states (Pennsylvania, Texas and New York named) slowing data-center permitting** — so **any AI cloud player not completely sold out can sell compute at very attractive prices**, and Arete expects compute tightness to last **at least through '28E**. Demand marker: Arete's Software-team GenAI tracker shows **Node.js SDK downloads up 9.2x y/y in July**, with only **17% open-source take-up in Arete's CIO surveys** — i.e. the workloads stay with the frontier labs. Affordability anchor: **frontier-lab revenue yield ~$50m per inference MW** ⚠️ (**also revenue/MW, but on a DIFFERENT denominator — the lab's own revenue over its inference MW, not the price CRWV charges — so it is a coverage ratio, never a comparable**), which is what Arete argues makes $20–25m/MW rents payable. **M&A tail: Arete thinks `IREN`'s power portfolio "could be extremely attractive" to CoreWeave, Nebius or SpaceX, and floats an all-stock merger as "extremely interesting" to CRWV.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260821_Arete_Research_NEO_Clouds-_Greater_Pricing_Power.html)

- **🆕 MANAGEMENT'S DIRECT ANSWER TO THE LOUDEST BEARS ON THIS PAGE, FROM THE GS COMMUNACOPIA PRIMARY** (Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference — **MANAGEMENT: Mike Intrator, CEO of CoreWeave** · Bloomberg LIVE transcript, 2026-09-08). On **inference migrating away to cheaper silicon**, CoreWeave says the question is malformed because it does not segment the fleet — "we don't really think about our infrastructure as being… training… and… inference. We think of it as AI infrastructure" (management's claim, UNVERIFIED — no workload split is disclosed). On **cost of capital**, he claims borrowing "at par or close to at par with many of the hyperscalers", which the MS 08-20 credit tape on this page contradicts by 300–600bp on offtake spreads while pricing CRWV's own BB term loan at 9.15%. On **hyperscalers retrenching**, he answers with a fact pattern and a mix argument — see § Customer concentration. ➤ **Net: the three structural bears are now answered on the record by the CEO, and two of the three answers are testable against data already on this page rather than against opinion.**

## Customer concentration & the OpenAI/hyperscaler web
- **OpenAI:** the break in OpenAI's compute exclusivity with Microsoft made CoreWeave a primary spillover beneficiary. OpenAI signed **two deals — an $11.9B multi-year contract plus a $4B expansion ($16B total over a 4–5yr timeframe)** — **and took an equity stake in CoreWeave**; the debt raise partly funds the build-out to service OpenAI (UBS OpenAI deep-dive, 2 Sep 2025).
- **Google Cloud:** Google itself signed a deal with CoreWeave that the press describes as offloading OpenAI workloads — i.e., CRWV is now sub-contracted compute for both OpenAI directly and via Google (UBS OpenAI deep-dive, 2 Sep 2025).
- **Microsoft loop:** much OpenAI spend on CoreWeave/Oracle is orchestrated on Azure software and reported partly as Azure revenue; Microsoft is also itself renting bare-metal GPU capacity from neoclouds to resell at thin margin (SemiAnalysis Microsoft AI Strategy, Dec 2025) — so the customer base is concentrated in a small, interlocking set of AI-infra buyers (Microsoft, OpenAI, Google, Meta).
- **Meta exposure — new headline risk (Citi Tech Research call, 2026-07-01):** Citi's summary (Alex Placeres/Ron Josey/Tyler Radke/Mike Rollins/Heath Terry, via Daniel Grozdea) sizes **~1/3 of CoreWeave's total backlog as tied to Meta** as a customer. Citi reads Meta's cloud pivot (see below) as making it **unlikely Meta signs new deals with CRWV going forward**, though strong overall AI demand may mitigate the long-term impact — Citi frames this explicitly as a **bear signal for neoclouds** (Citi Tech Research call, 2026-07-01). This is the first backlog-concentration figure specific to Meta in-corpus; prior framing (above, Nomura 2026-06-30) sized the Meta relationship in deal-value terms (~$21bn Apr-26 expansion to Dec-2032 + ~$14bn Sep-2025 deal) rather than as a share of total backlog — read the two alongside each other, not as a contradiction.
- **Meta "Meta Compute" pivot — read as a risk to the CRWV bull case (Barclays · Ross Sandler, Software team, 2026-07-01):** Bloomberg reported Meta is launching an external cloud business ("Meta Compute") to sell surplus AI computing capacity, similar to AWS Bedrock, positioning Meta to compete directly with AWS/Azure/GCP. Barclays flags this as a headline risk to neocloud names including CoreWeave — a hyperscaler customer becoming a compute-selling competitor changes the demand calculus for CRWV's own capacity. Barclays maintains **Equal Weight, PT $120** on CRWV (price $99.54, +20.6% upside) (Barclays · Ross Sandler, 2026-07-01). Separately, Barclays (2026-06-30) had noted Meta signed **$20bn+ TCV deals with neoclouds CoreWeave and Nebius**, plus multi-year deals with Oracle, Google Cloud and AWS — underscoring CRWV as a named, material Meta compute-supply counterparty even as the "Meta Compute" pivot raises the question of whether that relationship keeps growing (Barclays · Ross Sandler, 2026-06-30).
- **Deal economics context (Citi Tech Research call, 2026-07-01):** recent large AI compute deals have been struck at **$40–60bn annually per GW**, a 30–60%+ premium to spot; hyperscaler cash ROIC on AI investment is estimated at **~29%** (consistent in Q4 and Q1 per Heath Terry). Full-transcript color beyond the summary already on the page: Radke puts the likelihood Meta signs another deal with CRWV/Nebius at **"virtually zero, at least for the near term"**; Nebius carries a **similar dollar magnitude of Meta exposure** (with a backstop); and from a Radke/Rollins datacenter tour a few weeks prior (incl. a CoreWeave NJ facility), **CRWV still has "a good amount of 2027 capacity to sell and they're allocating it based on the highest price"** — a live demand-side offset to the Meta headline. Terry also notes the **spot market is only ~3–5% of the total compute market** (so spot weakness around big deals is a poor demand gauge) and "there is no such thing as a dark GPU" (Citi Tech Research replay call — Placeres/Josey/Radke/Rollins/Terry, 2026-07-01).
- **Bernstein Quick Take — Meta exposure quantified in dollars (Rezaei/Shmulik/Rasgon/Wu, "Meta's will they/won't they neocloud ambitions (situationships don't usually end well)", 2026-07-01):** reiterates **Underperform, PT $67** (close $99.54, −33% implied). Meta currently holds **$35.2B of CRWV contracts — "a little over a third of CRWV's backlog"**; layer on the **~$14B from Microsoft** and **"nearly half of CRWV's backlog comes from customers who will be full competitors at the time of renewal."** Bernstein sizes Meta's datacenter footprint at **~20 GW globally today with another ~14 GW coming online in the next few years** (Bernstein est. IT-load basis, owned + leased — do not mix with facility-GW figures) — scale that "easily rivals cloud provider footprints." Existing CRWV contracts are "tight and difficult to churn out of," so **no immediate risk to CRWV's financials or debt**, but the note reads the news as "extremely consistent" with the initiation thesis ("Hyperscalers are Situationships, Not Spouses"): larger, advantaged competitors enter GPUaaS and CRWV "will both lose their existing customers and struggle to compete for new ones" — adding to Google-Blackstone and Colossus; "the CRWV business model will be unsustainable." Four flagged unknowns: (1) "developing plans" ≠ launching; (2) re-selling may be temporary until Meta needs the capacity itself (note the mixed reports of Google throttling Meta compute); (3) service form matters — Meta-models-only API vs Bedrock/Vertex-style multi-model vs bare-metal (GPUs or MTIA?) have very different economics; (4) even a temporary Meta entry raises questions on 2027+ capital deployment across the space. PT basis: 28.4x 2027E Adj. EBIT/share of $5.81; ests. revenue $12.37B F26E / $19.73B F27E, EBITDA $6.23B / $11.74B, adj. EPS $(4.20) / $(2.41) (Bernstein Quick Take, 2026-07-01).
- **SemiAnalysis pushback — Meta is an RPO tailwind, not a threat (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02):** the direct bull counter to the Citi/Barclays/Bernstein/Rothschild Meta-Compute bear cluster above. SemiAnalysis calls the neocloud sell-off (CRWV/Nebius) on "overcapacity" fears **erroneous** — Meta's datacenter/compute procurement will **accelerate, not slow** (>5GW contracted across Cloud & Colo in 1H26; 2027 capex "shockingly high"). Key point for CRWV: after ~10GW of Meta deals since early-2024, the **bulk of Meta's capacity additions now come via 3rd parties**, so SemiAnalysis expects Meta to remain **"a huge source of RPO growth for the likes of CoreWeave, Nebius and others."** Meta's four high-value compute use-cases (MSL frontier training, RecSys >10x, private-Claude TaaS, SpaceX-type deals) mean it keeps contracting aggressively even if it also resells surplus. Read alongside the bear cluster, not as a contradiction of it — the debate is whether Meta's external-cloud pivot displaces or expands neocloud demand. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_META.html)
- **Bernstein announced-deal tally (Quick Take Exhibit 2, 2026-07-01)** — Bernstein's per-customer backlog build, useful as the in-corpus reference table: MSFT est. **~$14B** (2023+, ~7yr, "effectively ~9 contracts"); IBM est. **~$5B** (Jan-25, "several thousand GB200s"); **OpenAI total $22.4B** ($11.9B Mar-25 + up to $4B May-25 + **up to $6.5B second expansion, Sep-25** — Bernstein's tally runs above the ~$16B UBS framing on this page, the delta being the Sep-25 expansion); **NVDA up to $6.3B backstop** (will purchase unsold CRWV capacity, through 4/13/2032); **Meta total $35.2B** (up to $14.2B late-2025, 6–7yr + $21B Apr-26 through Dec-2032 incl. early Vera Rubin); Anthropic est. **<$2B** (Apr-26, phased — vs Cantor's ~$2–3B/70MW estimate on this page, cite both); Jane Street **$6B** multi-year plus $1B equity; all remaining customers **~$8.5B** off 1Q26 backlog (Bernstein Quick Take, 2026-07-01). ⚠️ **09-11 CONFLICT ON THE NVDA LINE, LOGGED NOT RESOLVED:** SemiAnalysis places the same **$6.3bn thru 2032** inside NVDA's **cloud service agreements** — NVDA's *own* in-house compute for model development and CI/CD, i.e. **NVDA as a paying tenant of CoreWeave**, explicitly distinguished from the AICP backstop line (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia's Backstop Universe – Heads I Win, Tails Who Loses?", 2026-09-11). Bernstein's "will purchase unsold CRWV capacity" and SemiAnalysis's "agreement… with CoreWeave" are the same size and end-date with opposite economics. **Neither is adopted; resolve off the 10-Q line-item language.**
- **🆕 NVIDIA AS A CUSTOMER, NOT ONLY AS A BACKSTOP — and the pool is modelled to grow (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia's Backstop Universe – Heads I Win, Tails Who Loses?", 2026-09-11; independent research, CRWV not a covered name — no rating/PT).** NVDA's **$29bn cloud-service-agreement line** covers its own model-development and CI/CD compute; the named CoreWeave piece is **$6.3bn through 2032**, and SemiAnalysis additionally believes NVDA **rents 10-20k GPUs from CoreWeave**, placing the balance against **~360MW in Batam via [[ORCL]], ~20MW at YTL in Malaysia and other Oracle facilities in the US**. They model the line **exiting F1/27 at $38bn and reaching $96bn by F1/31**. ➜ **For the concentration file this is a distinct counterparty type: an AA-rated tenant that is also the supplier — it diversifies the revenue roster away from the hyperscaler/lab axis while deepening the NVDA relationship on both sides of the P&L.** ⚠️ *Third-party estimate of a counterparty's disclosure, not a CRWV disclosure; CRWV has not broken out NVDA as a customer.*

- **Bernstein earnings preview — "what to watch" into the Q2 print (Madison via Tyler Seidman, 2026-07-09):** reiterates **Underperform, TP $67**. Expects a low-event 2Q; the real bogey is the **hefty 2H AOI expectation of $790M–$1.05B** (assuming 2Q finishes within the $30–90M AOI guide). Watch items: **power-delivery updates** (distribution slated for 2H this year per IR), **sequential margin expansion**, and **backlog visibility into 2027**. Notably, IR told Bernstein the **biggest growth constraint is FINANCING, not demand** — GPU/power availability stays constrained over the next 6–12 months with a more favorable backdrop only ~18 months away — pushing the crux of the debate onto the capital structure rather than demand. Management's prior "**margin pressure is timing-based, not economic**" framing (a powered shell absorbs lease/power/depreciation before contribution margins normalize ~by month 3) is the thing Bernstein wants validated by an **on-track ramp to 1.7GW by year-end** and an **upward AOI trajectory** confirming the sequential margin step-ups. Bernstein expects **2026 revenue within the previously-guided range**. Consistent with (not a change to) its standing Underperform/$67 above (Bernstein, 2026-07-09).
- **Neoclouds central to the "AI Project Trinity"; NVDA backstop de-risks GPU-cluster financing (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity", 2026-07-06):** the full paid note frames Neoclouds as the beneficiaries of NVDA's new **GPU rental backstop** — a **take-or-pay minimum-revenue guarantee** on the underlying GPU capacity (NVDA shares in Neocloud revenue above the backstop level; intent is it is never invoked). Lenders lean on NVDA's **AA/Aa2 IG** rating to fund clusters that otherwise couldn't raise debt outside a 5yr IG-hyperscaler-backstopped template — directly de-risking the **Capital** leg for Neoclouds and enabling **<5yr rental tenors** to a broader buyer base. Read-through for CRWV: (1) NVDA is already listed as a CRWV backstop counterparty (up to **$6.3B** backstop / purchase of unsold capacity through 4/13/2032, per Bernstein Exhibit 2 above), so the program formalizes a structure CRWV already has; (2) SemiAnalysis places CRWV at the **top of its ClusterMAX Gold tier** (the only industry Neocloud rating system it uses to underwrite lenders), reinforcing the operator-quality bull; (3) the note keeps the open debate live on **GPU residual value / lack of a derivatives market** and the **5-year offtake template** as the crux of lender risk-pricing — the same duration-mismatch/re-contracting-ASP debate that drives the CRWV depreciation bear case above. Bull for CRWV's financeability and NVDA-alignment moat; the residual-value/pricing debate is unresolved. (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop...", 2026-07-06)

- **The credit-pricing math behind the financing debate — CRWV's own curve, quantified (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity", 2026-07-06 — net-new detail from the full note, folded 2026-07-24):** the single most useful part of the note for this page, and it puts hard numbers on the "financing is the binding constraint, not demand" consensus (Bernstein/IR, 07-09). **CoreWeave's 5-year unsecured bonds yield ~10% (≈600bps Z-spread)** versus **5.9% on the fixed-rate tranche of the $8.5B DDTL 4.0 delayed-draw term loan, which was backstopped by Meta** — about **90bps wide of Meta's own ~5.0% 5-year bond yield**, which SemiAnalysis reads as "**the market pricing CoreWeave's execution risk with respect to that particular project at 90bps.**" That is a remarkably tight execution premium and a direct rebuttal to the "structurally disadvantaged by fixed debt" bear (Rothschild/Bernstein) *when a hyperscaler offtake is attached* — for reference, hyperscaler-backstopped 5-year deals price around **SOFR+225bps (~195bps Z-spread, ~5.9% all-in)**. The sensitivity that decides the argument: moving a top neocloud's all-in funding cost from **5.62% to 10% cuts PBT margin from 14.8% to 5.4%** — against **70-80% loan-to-value** on GPU financing, so the ~4% unsecured penalty is roughly two-thirds of the pre-tax margin. Lenders size loans to a **DSCR of at least 1.3x** (measured, notably, under the scenario where the backstop is *activated*), which is what produces those LTVs. Nvidia's new backstop program exists precisely to let neoclouds fund **shorter-tenor, more diversified client books without a hyperscaler offtake**; SemiAnalysis expects that lending to price **wider than the ~5.9% hyperscaler-backstopped deals but tighter than CRWV's ~10% unsecured** — i.e. the program's value to CRWV is measurable as the compression between those two marks. Two market-structure datapoints that cut in CRWV's favour on the demand side while confirming the bear on the cost side: it is still **a seller's market for short-dated rentals**, with some neoclouds requiring **prepays of up to 100% of contract value** (theoretically infinite IRR — a cluster stood up with no cash out the door), and **inference providers refuse tenors longer than 1 year while AI labs will sign 3+ years**; but **neocloud colocation leases carry a 3-5% higher yield-on-cost** than hyperscale/IG deals because of weaker credit plus forced amortisation/cash-sweep terms with ~3-year refinance windows — the landlord charges CRWV for exactly the credit gap the bond market prices. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-06) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_ENC_Nvidia_GPU_Debt_Backstop_Unleashes_the_AI_Project_Trinity_Capital_Offtake_and_Datacenters_email.html)
- **Novel treasury/risk move — CRWV exploring derivatives to hedge memory & storage chip prices (Reuters, per @jukan05 / @wallstengine / @carlquintanilla, 2026-07-15):** CoreWeave is reportedly exploring the use of **financial derivatives to hedge against a future drop in memory and storage chip prices** (Reuters, citing sources). Read-through: DRAM/NAND are at cycle highs (see [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md) / memory-pricing corpus), and CRWV — a heavy forward buyer of chips for its build-out — appears to want **downside protection on the value of future/inventory chip purchases** so a memory-price rollover doesn't strand it holding above-market componentry. Mixed signal: (bull) sophisticated treasury/risk management ahead of a potential memory-cycle turn, and confirmation CRWV thinks it is a large enough structural chip buyer to warrant hedging; (bear/context) implicitly concedes memory pricing is a real forward risk to component costs, and that CRWV is exposed to a chip-price down-cycle. Contextual read-through — **@Midnight_Captl (2026-07-15)** argues NVIDIA is poised to grow a "Compute Futures Market" moat: compute consumers (structurally short compute) will want to hedge input costs while producers hedge output prices, and liquidity will coalesce around NVDA-based futures — i.e. hedging instruments for AI-infra inputs are an emerging market CRWV would be an early user of. (@jukan05 / @wallstengine / @carlquintanilla, 2026-07-15; @Midnight_Captl, 2026-07-15.)
- **Strong appetite for CRWV's AI-infra debt, but duration-mismatch flagged (@theinformation, 2026-07-13):** Morgan Stanley's **$3.1B CoreWeave loan drew more than $19B in investor orders** — a ~6x oversubscription showing deep appetite for AI-infrastructure debt (mirrors the >$19B-orders demand and confirms CRWV as the reference credit for GPU-backed paper, consistent with the JPM "AI Capex 2.0" proof-of-concept framing above). The bear caveat in the same report: "the loans **may outlast the chips and customer contracts supporting them**" — a direct restatement of the duration-mismatch / GPU-residual-value / re-contracting-ASP debate that anchors the CRWV depreciation bear case (10yr+ paper vs ~3-7yr GPU economic life). Mixed: strong demand for the paper (bull for financeability) vs asset-life/contract-duration risk to lenders (bear). (@theinformation, 2026-07-13.)

- **🆕 THE HYPERSCALER-PULL-BACK BEAR, PUT TO THE CEO DIRECTLY AND ANSWERED WITH A FACT-PATTERN ARGUMENT** (Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference — **MANAGEMENT: Mike Intrator, CEO of CoreWeave** · Bloomberg LIVE transcript, 2026-09-08). Borges puts the standing bear in its investor form: when supply/demand normalises, hyperscaler customers "can simply then pull back what they've… quote unquote outsourced… and retrench back in the stack". The answer has two legs. **(1) The fact pattern:** "they build data centers and they could have pulled those back, but they didn't… They go through these periods where they build their own data centers, and then they go through these periods where they go to third party data center providers" — and on the IPO-era claim that the contracts were one-offs, "then they came back and did it five more times with us. And then they did it across the industry." **(2) The contracts bought TIME, and the hedge is a broadened base:** "what these contracts did for us was provide us with time… we have begun to win a broader and broader universe of clients… our ability to support enterprise, our ability to support governments, our ability to support our own products internally, our managed inference product… those internal demand sinks [transcript: "syncs"] are incredible paths to building a sustainable cloud." He concedes the downside explicitly: **"some of those contracts may come back to us, some of those contracts may not."** ➤ **He is NOT claiming renewal; he is claiming the base will be diversified enough by the time it matters. That is a falsifiable statement about MIX, and this page already holds the measuring stick (non-IG AI-native share of backlog, enterprise/government adds) — but it does not answer the roll-off-book sizing question Agrawal declined on 08-11.** ✅ **Origin note, relevant to the margin debate: he describes the business as having started "cost plus" — "we'll build this for you" and "we're willing to pay cost plus for it" — with scale since then "driving economics and driving margin, which… will continue to accrete to us over time".**

## Hedgeye model snapshot (HE Global Technology BB, Dec 2025)
Hedgeye estimates 2026 CoreWeave revenue ~**$11.68B (+126%)** on ~477k average GPUs across ~50 datacenters, implying **2026 revenue/GPU ~$24,487** (vs. Nebius ~$18,949). It pegs total EV ~**$92.8B** with debt incl. lease obligations ~**$50.2B** and cash (ex-restricted) ~$0.9B → ~7.9x 2026 EV/sales; it models a 2026 adjusted loss. Use as a directional bear model, not consensus.

Why it matters here: the AICP floor is set **below market on purpose — ~$2.35/hr/GPU for a GB300 over six years vs a $4.50-4.60 range for 5-year contracts** — and its stated purpose is to let non-IG operators borrow at IG pricing until *"customer revenues and independent capital fund subsequent expansion."* ➜ **Two things to track: (a) whether the pause holds, or NVDA restarts under the thinner 25%-residual-value capital-partnership template instead; (b) whether backstopped rivals (Firmus $21.1bn / 360MW Batam, SharonAI $4.2bn, GMI ~$2.2bn of NVDA obligation) show up bidding below CRWV's contract pricing while the floor is in force.** ⚠️ **Those $ figures are NVDA's maximum obligation, not revenue and not build cost.**

## Risks
- **Customer concentration:** ~67% of FY2025 revenue from top customer (Microsoft); ~65% of Q1-26 revenue from top two; OpenAI commitment ~$11.9B (plus $4B expansion, ~$16B total per UBS OpenAI deep-dive) through Oct-2030, Microsoft + Meta the other anchors; OpenAI also holds equity in CRWV (10-K FY2025; 10-Q Q1-26; UBS OpenAI deep-dive). The buyer base is a small interlocking AI-infra set — loss/reduction from any one would be material.
- **Substantial indebtedness:** $25.1B principal debt at high effective rates (10–15%), $536M quarterly interest expense, covenant-heavy non-recourse DDTL facilities; debt could divert cash flow and constrain flexibility (10-K/10-Q risk factors). Hedgeye flags a "liability surge" (debt incl. leases ~$50.2B by 2026YE on its model) as a de-rating risk.
- **Lease commitments:** leases all datacenters — $10.1B operating-lease ROU / $9.6B+ non-current lease liabilities; long-dated fixed obligations against contract-dependent revenue (10-Q Q1-26).
  - **How the datacenter (incl. BTC-miner-host) leases flow through the P&L (10-Q Q1-26, Leases note + MD&A):** the host deals are **operating leases under ASC 842** (datacenter initial terms 5–15 yrs + up-to-10-yr extension options, WA remaining term **11 yrs**, WA discount rate **10%**). Quarterly lease cost Q1-26: **$388M straight-line operating lease cost + $132M variable lease cost** (CAM, utilities/power reimbursed to landlord, security — expensed as incurred) **+ $19M finance-lease cost** ($11M ROU amortization + $8M interest) = **$539M total, vs $208M PY (+159% y/y, faster than revenue's +112%)**. Geography: rent + utilities sit in **cost of revenue** ($716M total in Q1) — i.e. **the host rent is INSIDE Adjusted EBITDA**; what Adj EBITDA excludes is the GPU/server D&A (in Technology & infrastructure, $1,273M) and the $536M interest below the line. That is the mechanical bridge from **56% Adj-EBITDA margin → ~1% Adj Op Income → $(740)M net loss**. Cash rent paid ($317M op-lease) ran **below** the $388M straight-line expense — escalating rent schedules front-load P&L expense vs cash in the ramp years. ROU assets jumped **$8.2B → $10.2B in one quarter** as new host capacity (CORZ et al.) reached lease commencement — each delivery adds a fixed 10%-discounted obligation before the GPUs on top of it earn revenue (the margin/timing mismatch below). (10-Q Q1-26, 2026-05-08, filed `CRWV\`; placed 2026-08-11 /wiki-query)
- **Margin/timing mismatch:** capacity costs commence before revenue ramps; near-term margin compression (Q4-25 / Q1-26 calls). The ~6yr GPU depreciation schedule is favorable but contested vs. ~4yr peer norm (MS AI Factories).
- **Pricing/re-contracting:** neocloud GPU prices have begun to fall like-for-like (Hedgeye); tail NPV depends on re-leasing at 50–75% of original ASP (DB) — a key downside lever if supply normalizes.
- **Supply / NVIDIA dependence:** GPU allocation and datacenter-provider delivery (single-provider Q4 delay flagged Q3-25); concentration in NVIDIA silicon.
  - **⚠️→✓ PARTIAL DE-RISKING OF THE DATACENTER-PROVIDER LEG, AND A NEW COST RISK ALONGSIDE IT — read-through from the Texas ERCOT audit (Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani, "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity", 2026-08-04).** Texas Governor Abbott has directed the PUCT and ERCOT to audit **all** data center projects in ERCOT's interconnection process, and **ERCOT has paused the "Batch Zero" review that was due to publish classification results on 2026-08-07**. **CoreWeave's contracted Core Scientific (CORZ) capacity is insulated from that delay: CORZ "has already delivered 437 MW to Coreweave… and remains on track to scale up to 590MW by early 2027. Thus, CORZ execution timelines are not impacted"** — the audit bites only on CORZ's **300 MW gross Pecos expansion**, not on the CRWV delivery schedule. Useful against the Bernstein bogey already on this page (the **1.7 GW year-end ramp** IR is being held to) — the largest single host relationship is not in the exposed bucket. ⚠️ **The offsetting read, and it is the more important one for CRWV: the note's whole thesis is that regulatory throttling of new interconnect capacity makes ALREADY-APPROVED MW MORE VALUABLE — "if MWs are scarce, it makes sense to earn more per MW" — evidenced by IREN's revenue yield per IT MW rising 50%, from ~$10mn under its Microsoft contract to ~$15mn under its NVIDIA contract, and enterprise cloud deals struck at GPU-hour rates 20-25% ABOVE previous guidance.** CoreWeave **leases every one of its datacenters** (see the lease-commitments risk above), so a rising $/MW for the landlord/host cohort is a **rising cost of capacity sourcing** — compounding the **3-5% higher yield-on-cost** SemiAnalysis already measures on neocloud colo leases (07-06) — while simultaneously validating pricing power on the sell side (consistent with the Baker contracted-base-repricing argument, 08-04). ⚠️ **Basis note: the $/IT MW and $/gross MW series in that note are separate and must not be mixed; full detail and the cohort table live on [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). CORZ, IREN, CIFR, WULF, RIOT, CLSK and MARA have no wiki pages — plain-text tickers only.** (Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani, 2026-08-04; CORZ covered by Madison Rezaei)
- **Widening net loss / debt-funded capex:** Q1 net loss **$(740)M**, more than double the prior-year quarter and the largest since going public, against **$31–35B** of FY2026 capex "funded primarily through debt" (Barron's "Inside Scoop," 2026-06-01) — the loss-widening and debt-funding combination compounds the balance-sheet risk already flagged above (10-K/10-Q risk factors).
- **Recurring insider selling into rallies:** a pattern across two consecutive Barron's issues, not a one-off — board members Karen Boone and Jack Cogen sold a combined **~$1.9M** as the stock "jumped 45% this year" (Barron's "Inside Scoop," 2026-06-01); the week prior, **3 insiders sold 349,000 shares for $44.372M** (week of 2026-05-11 to 2026-05-15, per the 13D insider-transactions table) (Barron's, 2026-05-18). Individually small, but the recurrence across both May and June prints is worth tracking as a sentiment tell even though it is not yet thesis-changing.

## In the inbox (Outlook — recent sell-side flow)
- **Cantor CoreWeave bull call (Cantor "CoreWeave call", 2026-06-18):** the cleanest in-corpus bull restatement into the 2Q print. Cantor models CRWV ending FY25 ~**$17B ARR → $30B FY26**, with **~75% of 2027's >$30B guide already contracted**; sees a **big RPO beat at 2Q** (consensus $104B; Cantor models updated total RPO ~**$125–130B** off the bond-memo $125B at 80% through the quarter — a 2nd straight quarter adding $30–40B TCV) **plus a likely sizeable new deal, "going to guess Anthropic"** (current Anthropic deal is only ~$2–3B / 70 MW, signed Apr 10/11 — "Anthropic isn't in the business of finding 70 MW"). Argues 2026 is sold-out/largely irrelevant and the **2027/28 estimates go up 15–20%** — run-rate EBITDA could be **~$25–26B in '27** (vs $18B). On the GPU-life bear: consensus moved from 3–4yr to **7–8yr**, Cantor thinks true useful life is **~10yr**; re-contracting holds because the original taker (e.g., Microsoft, 11 deals signed) renews. Spot vs contract pricing gap is wide — a 5yr GB300 contract ~**$3.50/GPU-hr** vs spot **~$7–9**; the Google–SpaceX deal at **~$10/GPU-hr for an H100** is above-market (Cantor reads it as Google passing compute to Anthropic into its IPO). CoreWeave's NVIDIA relationship (NVDA helps secure DC capacity + may backstop/finance GPUs to enable short-term/on-demand) is the differentiator vs other neoclouds; adding more spot/on-demand capacity is the next re-rating lever (Cantor "CoreWeave call", 2026-06-18).
- **Fluidstack / neocloud financing read-through (Wells Fargo "Fluidstack call", 2026-06-08):** off-coverage neocloud (FluidStack CEO Mike McDonald, deploying ~1 GW this year + multi-GW next) read-through to CRWV economics. Core point: **financing cost is the swing variable** — "if you're financing it at 6%, ship it; at high-teens, I'm less excited" (Oracle/CoreWeave named as having brought financing in-house). The **Anthropic Q1 inflection drove the sector's supply-demand imbalance**; inference is increasingly the interesting bit (Bedrock "is just 95% Claude" despite 30+ models). Inference economics approaching **human dollar-per-hour output** in vertical tasks (e.g., the Kirkland & Ellis $500M deal) is the demand-durability argument supporting CRWV's tail-value/re-contracting case (Wells Fargo "Fluidstack call", 2026-06-08).
- **Jefferies conference desk color (Jefferies "Brent Thill AI", 2026-06-01):** "**CoreWeave absolutely crushing** … they're sold out"; a top-3 AI company told the desk "we need tokens and can't find enough … we cannot find enough infrastructure" — corroborates the sold-out/demand-exceeds-supply thesis (Jefferies "Brent Thill AI software/infra", 2026-06-01).
- **Bernstein Underperform PT $67** + **Rothschild debt-encumbrance call** _(briefings, 2026)_: $99.4B backlog vs ~$25B high-coupon debt, $536M/qtr interest, ~65% top-2 customer concentration; FY26 AOI guide called 'nearly unhittable.'
- **Nomura (Asia AI Semi & Server, 2026-06-30, Not rated):** frames neoclouds as CSPs' preferred fast-access compute during the GB300→VR200 transition (shorten lead time, faster access to newest chips, demand-erosion risk mitigation, financial flexibility). New contract specifics on CRWV: **Meta expanded its CoreWeave deal in Apr-26 to a long-term AI-cloud capacity agreement running to Dec 2032, ~$21bn deal value** (initial deployments NVIDIA Vera Rubin) — on top of the earlier Sep-30-2025 Meta–CoreWeave ~$14bn deal through 2031-32; and CoreWeave signed a **multi-year Anthropic agreement (Apr-26), a phased infrastructure rollout with room to expand**. Records the CRWV–NVIDIA 5GW GPU commitment through 2030 (NVDA $2bn invested) as a ceiling (Nomura, 2026-06-30).

CRWV's OWN cost of debt is improving; the price of ITS CREDIT AS AN OFFTAKER is not. Those are two different curves and the thesis needs both. **Q3'26 synthesis (opened 2026-08-11):** the debate has moved. For four quarters this page's central question was *"can margins inflect before the debt does?"* — the Q2 print answers **yes, earlier than either bulls or bears modelled** (adj. OM ~5% vs ~3% bogey, EBITDA 59%). Two questions replace it: (1) **does it compound** — JPM's 7.0% vs 7.5% F3Q consensus is the near-term scoreboard; and (2) **does the funding cost fall** now that [[NVDA]]'s $500bn third-party financing platform exists and, per @firstadopter, is aimed at neoclouds rather than hyperscalers. The unglamorous risk is unchanged: **$9.4B of quarterly capex against $679M of CFO**, i.e. the thesis is still financed, not funded. **🆕 Updated 2026-08-12 once the primary transcript was read: question (2) is now half-answered by the company itself, and question (1) has to be restated. On funding cost, management disclosed a ~300bp reduction in weighted-average cost of debt over the past year ≈ $1.1B of annualized interest saving — the first hard number on the variable both bulls and bears agree is binding. On margin, the inflection is SEQUENTIAL ONLY: adjusted operating income rose from $21M to $128M q/q but FELL from $200M y/y, while interest expense went $267M → $640M and is guided to $860–940M in Q3. None of the four same-day broker notes carried the interest line. So the honest scoreboard for Q3 is not "7.0% vs 7.5% operating margin" alone — it is whether PF operating income of $200–260M closes any of the gap to $860–940M of interest. Third question added: how big is the roll-off book? Management declined to size it ("a very limited part of our fleet"), so the recontracting-ASP debate that the A100-to-2029 quote is being used to settle remains unquantified.** **🆕 UPDATED 2026-08-20 — question (2) now has an EXTERNAL answer, and it is split. On CRWV's own paper the answer is YES, the funding cost is falling: MS Credit has the CRWV term loan (S+550, BB, 9.15%) tightening 584→527, the largest move in its leveraged-loan table, and CRWV now issuing EUR high yield ($2bn) — independent corroboration of the ~300bp WACD compression management claimed. But the same note prices CRWV OFFTAKE 300–600bp behind [[AMZN]] offtake on physically comparable data-center projects (APLD 8.2% / CORZ 11.5% / ELKGVP 12.0% / EDGCOM 14.2% vs STNGRY 7.9–8.8%) — so the borrower is getting cheaper while the counterparty is not, and the second curve is the one Bernstein's structural bear lives on. THAT SPREAD IS THE NEW BEST SCOREBOARD ON THIS PAGE: it is observable, dated, and it resolves an argument that four quarters of equity-side opinion has not. A FOURTH question is now added, and it is the one the page has no answer to: what happens to CRWV's scarcity value as the neocloud field goes from two credible names to *"hundreds"* (JPM Asia, 08-20) / 20 buying optical networks (Wolfe, 08-14)? Falling customer concentration and falling operator scarcity are the same event seen from two sides. And one uncomfortable supplier-side observation to carry into it: receivables days rose materially at Super Micro when CoreWeave and xAI were larger shares of its sales (Wolfe, 08-14) — CRWV takes prepays from customers and stretches suppliers, which is good treasury and a poor counterparty profile at once.** **🆕🔴 Updated 2026-08-21 — A THIRD QUESTION IS NOW ON THE TABLE, AND IT INVERTS THE LEVERAGE DEBATE: (3) *is the leverage the bear case or the bull case?* Arete answers "bull", and does it with the same balance sheet the bears cite — because CRWV's DDTL contract-financing stack makes its EQUITY value smaller than Nebius's against slightly greater near-term unsold GW, CRWV is the SECOND-most levered listed neocloud to rising compute pricing (behind `IREN`, ahead of [[NBIS]]), with unsold MW per $bn of market cap of 50 at YE28E vs Nebius's 33.** That is the first framing on this page in which "financed, not funded" is an argument FOR owning the equity rather than against it, and it arrives with the highest PT the page has carried (**BUY, $317, raised from $303, revenue 26%/37% above consensus in '28E/'29E**). ⚠️ **It does not retire question (1) or (2) — it re-prices them: the same fixed-debt structure that amplifies upside on repricing is what the credit market is charging 300–600bp for on the offtake side, and Arete's own timing caveat is that the new pricing reaches CRWV mainly through GPU contracts maturing from 4Q26 onward, not this quarter** (Arete Research "NEO Clouds: Greater Pricing Power", 2026-08-21). **🆕 09-06 update — CoreWeave is the named first recipient of production Vera Rubin.** [[DELL]]'s CEO, first-party: ***"We have delivered the world's first production NVIDIA Vera Rubin NVL72 racks to CoreWeave"*** (@MichaelDell, relayed by @dnystedt, 2026-09-06). This is an **allocation** datapoint sourced from the OEM rather than from CRWV's IR, and it closes out the ramp-risk flag this page carried from SemiAnalysis in the Q1'26 window (Vera Rubin racks *"still 'Eng/Quality Samples'"*). ⚠️ **It is bounded to exactly that: no rack count, no MW, no dollar value, no revenue-service date, and therefore no read across to the ~4.2 GW contracted mark, the $104bn backlog or the adj.-capex line. The live test is whether first-in-line on the new platform converts into the pricing this page is tracking (~$16M/MW/yr on long-term contracts) at the Q3'26 print, or is simply a delivery-sequence courtesy.**

| **08-25** | 🔴 **SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel podcast w/ Daniel Grozdea** (annual long-form; ⚠️ **one practitioner’s forward estimates delivered on a podcast — not company guidance, not consensus, no rating or PT**) | capital / preco | **mixed** | **READ-THROUGH — no CRWV mark, but the clearest statement on this page of the POWER STRUCTURE a contract-first neocloud operates inside, and it is not flattering.** 🔴 **Verbatim: “For compute that you can build without financing… If Meta can build compute, Microsoft, Amazon, SpaceX can build compute WITHOUT FINDING A CUSTOMER, just saying, fuck it, I’m gonna build this compute, and then turn around and wait till it’s already built, THEY NOW CONTROL WHAT’S GOING ON. So most compute is contracted well before it’s built… Other people, you go to a random cloud, they’re like, okay, I’m going to build a gigawatt of compute… I need to turn around and FIND A CUSTOMER. If I want to find a customer, I need to find the CAPITAL. Who’s going to give me the capital on the customer? THE CUSTOMER HAS TO SIGN A DEAL. And then I take the customer’s commitment to the credit markets and I raise the capital.”** ➜ **That is precisely CoreWeave’s model described from the outside, and it names the structural asymmetry: balance-sheet builders capture the option value of unsold capacity in a rising market; contract-first builders sold that option to the counterparty when they signed. It is the same asymmetry the [[META]] and SpaceX “hoarding” row on the META page describes from the other side.** 🔴 **THE PRICE CALL THAT MATTERS FOR THE 2027 RATE DEBATE: asked what compute sold at 40 (US$bn/GW) transacts for at the end of next year, Patel says “I think MOST COMPUTE WILL STILL CONTINUE TO TRANSACT AT SUB $20 BILLION A GIGAWATT” — even at end-2027 — “BECAUSE ALL OF IT HAS TO BE FINANCED.” Financing, not demand, is what caps the realised price for the contract-first cohort. Against that, he expects the LABS to have to pay “25, 30, 50 million dollars a megawatt” to take 70-80% of incremental compute in 2028.** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE: every $/GW and $/MW figure here is REVENUE per unit of capacity per year, not build cost, not capex and not debt. Print is 2026-08-11 AMC.** | **guidance / margem** | **bull, with the margin concession stated up front** | **★ THE ONLY PUBLISHED PREVIEW ON THE PAGE FOR THE 08-11 PRINT, and it is unusually explicit about what it expects to go wrong. "Fundamentally, we are VERY BULLISH and think the pullback from May/June levels presents a VERY ATTRACTIVE ENTRY POINT from a 12-month+ perspective, but ACKNOWLEDGE CONCERNS AROUND MARGINS AMID RAMPING SCALING COSTS AND ONGOING FINANCING NEEDS MAY REMAIN STICKING POINTS FOR SOME NEAR-TERM."** 🔴 **THE CHECKABLE BOGEYS, which is what makes this useful rather than promotional: "For C2Q itself, we view MSD%+ REVENUE UPSIDE as a reasonable expectation and anticipate ACTIVE POWER ADDITIONS TAKING A LARGER +250–350MW Q/Q STEP-UP VS THE ~150MW ADDED IN THE PRIOR QUARTER. At a minimum, this should support A REITERATION OF FULL-YEAR REVENUE AND ACTIVE POWER TARGETS as large new capacity blocks appear to be tracking ON, OR IN SOME CASES SLIGHTLY AHEAD OF, SCHEDULE."** ⚠️ **A 250–350MW q/q addition versus ~150MW prior is a specific, falsifiable number and the single cleanest thing to check on 08-11.** ⚠️ **THE MARGIN MECHANIC, and it is a genuine near-term negative stated by the bull: "The FLIP-SIDE TO RAPID CAPACITY GROWTH IS NEAR-TERM MARGIN PRESSURE FROM COSTS STARTING TO COME ONLINE AHEAD OF REVENUE (e.g. LEASES, POWER AND DEPRECIATION); we believe this LIMITS OPPORTUNITY FOR UPSIDE TO NON-GAAP OPERATING MARGINS THAT WE NOW MODEL REMAINING IN THE LSD% – MSD% RANGE THROUGH C3Q BEFORE STARTING TO INFLECT IN C4Q."** So: low-to-mid single-digit non-GAAP operating margins for two more quarters, inflecting only in Q4 — anyone owning this into the print should not expect margin relief. **On capex, and note the definitional point matters: "We DON'T ANTICIPATE A FURTHER CHANGE TO FULL-YEAR CAPEX GUIDANCE, absent some acceleration/pull-forward of capacity, after last quarter's increase ATTRIBUTED TO COMPONENT COSTS, and REMIND INVESTORS THE COMPANY DEFINES CAPEX BASED ON WHEN ASSETS ARE PLACED INTO REVENUE SERVICE, NOT ON CASH OUTLAYS."** ⚠️ **That definition is a trap for cross-company capex comparisons — CoreWeave's capex line is not on the same basis as a hyperscaler's cash capex.** 🔴 **THE DIRECT REBUTTAL TO THE OVERBUILD BEAR THIS WINDOW CREATED: "Any concern about [[SPCX]] AND [[META]] SELLING 'EXCESS CAPACITY' EXTERNALLY SIGNALING RISK OF AN OVERBUILD SEEMS MISPLACED"** — worth holding against [[ORCL]]'s own bear list from UBS the same week, which names SPCX, META and OpenAI-via-Pike-County as competitors selling/consuming compute. **Two houses, same facts, opposite conclusions; both now on the wiki.** Zelnick's four bull pillars into the back half and the late-September user conference start with "SUPPORTIVE CONTRACTING ENVIRONMENT" and centre on "underlying ROIC and the DIFFERENTIATION AND BREADTH of CoreWeave's portfolio" becoming apparent, on shares ">30% OFF MAY HIGHS." **Positioning note: Roundhill launched a NEOCLOUD ETF on 2026-08-06 with CRWV at 27.30% (second weight behind [[NBIS]] at 30.83%; IREN 7.72%, HUT 6.32%, WULF 4.97%) — a new marginal, price-insensitive holder of the equity (@ParadisLabs, 2026-08-06).** (Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick / Bhavin Shah / Daniel Knauff / Yash Kejriwal, 2026-08-06) |
| 07-22 | **★ AlphaSense expert call — Former Senior Director of Product (hyperscaler + neo-cloud GTM/deal-structuring; names CoreWeave as first-hand experience) — "Hyperscalers Hold Strategic Advantages in AI Infrastructure Investments"** | valuation | **bear** | ⚠️ **Dating and provenance first: PUBLISHED 2026-07-22 but INTERVIEWED 2026-04-23 — a three-month-old view, filed by AlphaSense under primary company GOOGL. It is routed here because CoreWeave, not Google, is the concrete subject: the expert speaks from direct CoreWeave experience ("Even at CoreWeave when I was we started it…") and the note is structurally a hyperscaler-vs-neocloud comparison.** 🔴 **THE PAYBACK SPREAD, which is the whole bear case in one number: _"When I say hyperscalers I'm including AWS, Microsoft, and Google… If I look at somebody like a hyperscaler, you are looking at a THREE TO FIVE payback on GPU. The infrastructure build is attractive for them because they can MONETIZE THE SAME ASSET ACROSS MULTIPLE REVENUE STREAMS. They can accept longer direct paybacks, because the ECOSYSTEM ROI IS BROADER. When you look at NEOCLOUDS, we look at TWO TO THREE YEARS PAYBACK PERIOD."_** And the explicit rule of thumb: _"if you're looking at an agile hyperscaler, STRONG PAYBACK IS LESS THAN THREE YEARS. Acceptable would be 3-5, RISKY WOULD BE MORE THAN FIVE, unless it's very strategic in nature. When I look at a new cloud, I would want to pay back in TWO TO THREE YEARS MAX ACCEPTABLE. THE RISK IS GREATER THAN THREE AND A HALF OR FOUR YEARS."_ ⚠️ **The asymmetry is the point and it is structural, not cyclical: the same asset, bought at the same price, must pay back roughly TWICE AS FAST at a neocloud because there is only one revenue stream against it — and the expert names the aggravating factors as _"more sensitive to DEBT COST, or even CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION, or GPU RESIDUAL… they need more CASH CONVERSION to support the growth and the obligation."_ That is the [[CRWV]] bear case stated by a practitioner rather than a short-seller, and it maps onto every leg this page already tracks (leverage, [[MSFT]]/[[META]] concentration, residual-value risk on contracted fleets).** **The mitigant the expert volunteers, and it is CoreWeave-specific: _"training clusters. Can they justify longer payback if backed… if I committed multi-contracts or customers like META DID WITH COREWEAVE?"_ — i.e. a large committed anchor contract is the recognised mechanism for stretching an acceptable neocloud payback, which is precisely what this page's Meta and OpenAI backlog items are.** 🔴 **SECOND ITEM — THE SCARCITY PREMIUM IS EXPECTED TO COMPRESS, and this is the forward call: the expert _"expects the market to shift toward MORE COMMITTED, LONG-TERM CONTRACTS for GPU usage as AI workloads mature and SUPPLY INCREASES, REDUCING THE SCARCITY PREMIUM seen in earlier cycles,"_ with the revenue mix _"shifting from predominantly HOURLY/SPOT RENTALS during periods of GPU scarcity to a higher proportion of COMMITTED, LONG-TERM CONTRACTS."_ Contract terms cited: one-, three- and five-year deals, _"longer-term contracts offer greater discounts."_** ⚠️ **Two-sided for this page rather than simply bearish: committed contracts are exactly what lengthens acceptable payback and de-risks the model, but the mechanism getting there is PRICE. Set it against the Gavin Baker datapoint on [[NVDA]] that B200 rentals ROSE 50-60% in six-to-seven months, and against the ~20-25%-over-18-months post-generation decay rate from the AWS expert file on [[AMZN]] — this call is a three-month-old forecast of compression, not an observation of it.** **Third: the KPI set the expert says is actually run — _"GPU utilization… realized price per GPU hour, then the fleet mix, the power plus the hosting cost, the contracted revenue mix, and the DEPLOYMENT LAG"_ — with monitoring reframed away from raw busy-ness toward _"how many PROFITABLE TOKENS, the workloads, the model improvements are produced per GPU purchase. That is where the true capital efficiency sits,"_ on biweekly-to-monthly rolling forecasts.** On custom silicon (relevant to the [[GOOG]]/[[AMZN]] competitive leg, not to CRWV directly): _"Amazon… already looking at using their custom silicon for Trainium, Inferentia… GOOGLE IS ONLY LOOKING AT TPU. Microsoft Silicon helps ensure that they're not remaining dependent solely on third-party GPUs"_ — but framed as _"LESS ABOUT REPLACING GPUs OVERNIGHT"_ and as _"a strategic and financial lever… NOT AN IMMEDIATE REPLACEMENT for established GPU providers."_ ⚠️ **Weight the whole call as a three-month-stale practitioner framework, strong on MECHANISM and unreliable on TIMING.** (AlphaSense expert call, published 2026-07-22, interviewed 2026-04-23) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GOOGL_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_05-08-2026.html) |

That figure would be MORE THAN WHAT ANALYSTS ESTIMATE COREWEAVE WILL GENERATE NEXT YEAR."** A two-year-old company projecting more 2027 revenue than CoreWeave either resets the group's growth ceiling or discredits neocloud projections generally — and the specific weakness in the number is visible: **the West Virginia complex (acquired March, via American Intelligence & Power, with permits and power agreements) "still would need TENS OF BILLIONS of dollars in financing… and IT'S YET TO ANNOUNCE A TENANT FOR THAT SITE."** **The playbook is explicitly CoreWeave's: Nscale is buying software startup Anyscale for $1.6bn, which the article likens directly to "CoreWeave's purchase of Weights and Biases… for $1.7 billion, just before CoreWeave went public in March 2025."** Backers: **>$6bn raised from 8090 Industries, Citadel and Jane Street; board additions Sheryl Sandberg and Nick Clegg.** ⚠️ **Tape context the article supplies, which cuts against the timing: "Stock of CoreWeave has PLUNGED 25% IN THE PAST MONTH," and Australian neocloud Sharon AI — +60% since its February IPO — has "CRATERED 42% in the past month" (Situational Awareness held Sharon as of June). Lambda hired banks last September targeting 1H26 and delayed; Crusoe has been talking to bankers.** |
| 07-31 | **GS · Katherine Murphy / Michael Ng — AI Project Pulse (July 2026)** | capital | **mixed** | **★ The structural item, and it changes how the whole neocloud group should be underwritten: "Increasing adoption of GPU BACKSTOP PROGRAMS across neoclouds. Chipmakers like NVDA are providing its neocloud customers with a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on underlying GPU capacity (in the event that the neocloud is unable to lease out its capacity to other customers) in exchange for A SHARE OF THE NEOCLOUD'S REVENUE ABOVE A CERTAIN LEVEL." NVDA named sovereign neoclouds FIRMUS and SHARON AI as early partners in the revenue-sharing and credit-support model.** ⚠️ **This is a collar written by the supplier on its own customers' demand risk: downside truncated, upside shared away. Two consequences worth carrying — (a) reported neocloud capacity and order flow are no longer clean reads on independent end-demand, since ordering can be sustained by the backstop; and (b) it advantages backstopped competitors relative to a CoreWeave that raises on its own credit, while concentrating the sector's credit chain on NVDA.** GS reads it positively for compute availability and for [[DELL]]/[[SMCI]]. **The same pattern is appearing beyond NVDA: Google is reportedly providing a CREDIT BACKSTOP plus CHIPS for an Anthropic 1.6GW / $15bn Texas campus** (WSJ, 2026-07-30). **Vendor-financed AI capacity is becoming a category, not a set of one-offs.** |

Same size, same end-date, opposite economics — contingent demand in one reading, contracted revenue from an AA-rated tenant in the other. AND THE PROGRAMME IS REPORTEDLY PAUSED — a direct read on the financing environment for CRWV's COMPETITORS, not for CRWV: NVDA backstops GPU rental contracts for six years at *"approximately $2.35/hr/GPU on average for the GB300 vs the $4.50 to $4.60 range for 5y contracts."*** Three deals announced — **Firmus 360MW Batam = $21.1bn of NVDA obligation, SharonAI $4.2bn, GMI ~$2.2bn (SemiAnalysis estimate)** — and **NVDA has *"reportedly paused AICP"* roughly two weeks before the note**, with the authors' own read being *"we do understand that Nvidia is not advancing any further AICP deals at this time."* The stated purpose is the competitive point: get non-IG operators borrowing at IG pricing and build a track record until *"customer revenues and independent capital fund that bought Baa2 paper on an AA tenant will not consent to a B-rated one."* ⚠️ *Directional contrast only, different instruments: this page's own credit marks (MS leveraged-loan table, 08-20) quote than earn.* ⚠️ **UNIT HAZARD: every per-capacity figure in this note (AICP ~$58.6M/MW ≈ $59bn/GW, PORTS-Pike ~$24.7M/MW, the residual-value structure ~$9.4bn/GW) is MAXIMUM OBLIGATION /
